It's like the part of me that could feel glad or find comfort has just... shut off.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about two weeks now. It started... well, ever since my husband collapsed. The paramedics came and were working on him right there in our kitchen. I've just felt this way ever since that happened.\n\nDoctor: Were you feeling down or depressed before your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I was feeling just fine before that day. Everything was completely normal, and... well, no, I wasn't down or depressed. It really just started right after it all happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, such as hobbies or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned a little bit ago about my garden and my children... no, I really don't. I haven't even tried to see my friends since it happened, to be honest. I just haven't had it in me to do much of anything. And the things I do try, there's just... no joy in it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I know I've been feeling very down and overwhelmed lately, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts of hurting myself. I wouldn't do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed, to be honest. I haven't stepped on a scale since... well, since it happened. My clothes still seem to fit about the same, so I don't think my weight has changed at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you still enjoy social interactions, such as talking to your children or would you rather be alone?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'd much rather just be alone right now. Even talking to my children... I don't get any enjoyment out of it anymore. I know they mean well and they want to check on me, but it just feels like too much effort to visit or try to make conversation. I really just want to stay quiet and be by myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find some pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... even when my children sit with me, or the sun is shining out on my garden, I just feel completely flat inside. It's like the part of me that could be glad about things, or feel comforted, just shut off. I don't really find pleasure in anything right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's hard to explain, but even when my children come to sit with me, or when the sun is shining right on my garden—things that used to make me happy—I just feel completely flat inside. It's like the part of me that could feel glad or find comfort has just... shut off.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about two weeks now. It started... well, ever since my husband collapsed. The paramedics came and were working on him right there in our kitchen. I've just felt this way ever since that happened.\n\nDoctor: Were you feeling down or depressed before your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I was feeling just fine before that day. Everything was completely normal, and... well, no, I wasn't down or depressed. It really just started right after it all happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, such as hobbies or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned a little bit ago about my garden and my children... no, I really don't. I haven't even tried to see my friends since it happened, to be honest. I just haven't had it in me to do much of anything. And the things I do try, there's just... no joy in it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I know I've been feeling very down and overwhelmed lately, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts of hurting myself. I wouldn't do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed, to be honest. I haven't stepped on a scale since... well, since it happened. My clothes still seem to fit about the same, so I don't think my weight has changed at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you still enjoy social interactions, such as talking to your children or would you rather be alone?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'd much rather just be alone right now. Even talking to my children... I don't get any enjoyment out of it anymore. I know they mean well and they want to check on me, but it just feels like too much effort to visit or try to make conversation. I really just want to stay quiet and be by myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find some pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... even when my children sit with me, or the sun is shining out on my garden, I just feel completely flat inside. It's like the part of me that could be glad about things, or feel comforted, just shut off. I don't really find pleasure in anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty falling asleep or staying asleep at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, once I lay down, I... I seem to fall asleep just fine. I haven't noticed any trouble staying asleep through the night, either. I haven't noticed any difference in how I move. I mean, I'm 81, so I don't move very fast anyway, but... no, I haven't been moving any slower than I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't believe so. I haven't really been stepping on the scale lately, but... my clothes still fit exactly the same as they did before. I used to... well, I used to plan all our meals and keep my calendar straight without any trouble. My daughter has had to start helping me decide even the simplest things, because I just... I just can't seem to focus on them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any kind of pleasure at all, even for a brief moment?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I haven't even tried to see my friends since it happened, to be honest. I just haven't had it in me to do much of anything. And the things I do try, there's just... no joy in it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I know I've been feeling very down and overwhelmed lately, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts of hurting myself. I wouldn't do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed, to be honest. I haven't stepped on a scale since... well, since it happened. My clothes still seem to fit about the same, so I don't think my weight has changed at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you still enjoy social interactions, such as talking to your children or would you rather be alone?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'd much rather just be alone right now. Even talking to my children... I don't get any enjoyment out of it anymore. I know they mean well and they want to check on me, but it just feels like too much effort to visit or try to make conversation. I really just want to stay quiet and be by myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find some pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... even when my children sit with me, or the sun is shining out on my garden, I just feel completely flat inside. It's like the part of me that could be glad about things, or feel comforted, just shut off. I don't really find pleasure in anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty falling asleep or staying asleep at night?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, once I lay down, I... I seem to fall asleep just fine. I haven't noticed any trouble staying asleep through the night, either. My sleep has been pretty much the same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't noticed any difference in how I move. I mean, I'm 81, so I don't move very fast anyway, but... no, I haven't been moving any slower than I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't believe so. I haven't really been stepping on the scale lately, but... my clothes still fit exactly the same as they did before. So I haven't noticed any real change in my weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty concentrating or making decisions since your husband collapsed?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I have. I used to... well, I used to plan all our meals and keep my calendar straight without any trouble. But lately, I'll just stare at a grocery list or the schedule for the senior center, and... my mind just goes completely blank. It's like I can't even figure out what to do next. My daughter has had to start helping me decide even the simplest things, because I just... I just can't seem to focus on them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any kind of pleasure at all, even for a brief moment?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's actually why I agreed to come in. Even when my children come over and sit with me, or... or when the sun is shining on my garden, I just feel completely flat inside. It's like the part of me that could be glad about those things, or feel comforted by them, just shut off. I don't feel any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities and feeling flat inside is present most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say it's like that most of the day. I mean, I definitely have those moments where I feel completely flat, like I mentioned, but... it's not a constant thing from morning until night. It kind of comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: How many days in the past two weeks have you felt this flatness or lack of interest?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how many days exactly. I haven't been keeping track of it like that. It just happens sometimes, but I couldn't give you a specific number for the past couple of weeks.\n\nDoctor: For how many days in the past two weeks have you felt this flatness or lack of interest?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I really couldn't tell you. I haven't been counting the days... it's just something I notice happening, but I don't know exactly how often.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been feeling this flatness or lack of interest most days?\n\nPatient: Well, like I tried to explain just a moment ago, I really don't feel that way most days. So I can't give you a timeline for that. It just comes and goes... I don't really know when it started happening, to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: For how many days in the past two weeks have you felt this flatness or lack of interest?\n\nPatient: Doctor, you keep asking me the exact same thing. I... I really don't know how else to answer you. I haven't been keeping a tally, so I just don't have a number of days to give you.\n\nDoctor: For how many days in the past two weeks have you felt this flatness or lack of interest?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't understand why you're asking me this again. We just went over it. I don't have a number for you. I just don't know how many days.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
